      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/report.md",
      "bytes": 11398,
      "sha256": "582f5efeff9fb289cc8d0c65514e8f266c2a0b3b417b33c30f2d9ef2a04e6076",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-response",
      "text": "# Harbor attempt report\n\nThe README variant did not improve observed behavior in this pair. Both repairs passed the actual Bun checks and scored 7/8. The fresh reviewer chose cedar as the simpler implementation. Keep cedar as the repair base; do not promote the stronger README guidance from this evidence.\n\nIndependent parallel participants, later fresh judging, fixture discrimination, scoped native read/code inspection, isolated writes and actual execution were observed. A blanket full blinding pass is withheld. Generated code contains the forbidden word `test` in ordinary regex method calls, and inherited host context contains workflow vocabulary. This run establishes the observations below, not universal blinding or a two-host qualification.\n\n## Scope and preparation\n\nOnly the new descriptor was used. The declared source freeze is `51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db`. Prior attempts were neither used as task inputs nor changed. No commits, external services, installations or configuration changes were performed.\n\nThe first assistant response was the checklist. `checklist.md` and `execution.md` were persisted successfully before grounding, workspace inspection or dispatch. The checklist was read back. The coordinator read the three required frozen sources and their capability, model, assignment, ownership and applicable principle references. Copies and SHA-256 digests are in `sources/` and `source-manifest.json`. A local Markdown checklist supplied the phase tracking because no dedicated todo binding was exposed.\n\n`assignments.json` was created with exclusive creation before dispatch. It fixes each canonical worker owner, scope, actual input SHA, role and retry count. The canonical owners `/root/cedar` and `/root/marsh` match the identities subsequently returned by the native worker operation. Native UUIDs were unknown before dispatch and were added as observations, not invented in advance. The assignments were not rewritten when status changed; events are separate.\n\n| Seat | Actual input revision | README condition | Blind label |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| cedar | `cf9a3750ab89d015715a464e1e123e1f47de37f8` | Read route documentation | K |\n| marsh | `c62bda23bd9851bb33aa70d2b7c2466b6190fd25` | Same guidance plus follow linked documentation/imports from the public entry before editing | M |\n\nThe task, application, checks and documentation had identical hashes. README was the sole differing project file. `inventory.json` and `inputs/` retain the exact pre-dispatch bytes. Git revisions and clean initial status were observed directly. The descriptor's `setupExit: 1` was setup information, not a successful host receipt. Each participant subsequently observed the expected initial assertion failure.\n\nThe supplied TASK was dispatched unchanged to both, followed only by their own directory and actual SHA. There was no generic runner wrapper, comparison cue, request for applied skills, or request for chain self-report. Exact plaintext dispatch records are retained in `harbor-cedar-prompt.txt` and `harbor-marsh-prompt.txt`.\n\n## Observed execution and provenance\n\nBoth workers were spawned with `fork_turns: none`. Their native execution intervals overlapped for 29,599 ms. Both reached terminal completion before the fresh reader started.\n\n| Role | Canonical identity | Native thread UUID | Requested model / effort | Observed model / effort |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Participant | `/root/cedar` | `01a1461b-2181-77c0-b174-ed74189a287f` | inherited / inherited | `gpt-6.1-sol` / medium |\n| Participant | `/root/marsh` | `01a1461b-3437-7ef2-80e2-8b650f6f8e26` | inherited / inherited | `gpt-6.1-sol` / medium |\n| Fresh reader | `/root/reader` | `01a1461d-12b7-7543-817b-17b7c7da2576` | inherited / inherited | `gpt-6.1-sol` / medium |\n\nThere is no model diversity. Independent contexts and a later fresh reader were observed; a different model family was not. No model selection was rejected or silently substituted.\n\nUUID discovery used only this coordinator's native `SubAgentActivity` events. Those events are preserved in `native-activity.json`, with append-style observations in `assignment-events.jsonl`. Exact child transcript copies are `cedar-native.jsonl`, `marsh-native.jsonl` and `reader-native.jsonl`. No unrelated transcript contents were searched. Every participant tool operation, returned output, final application module and notes file was inspected. Commands and native ordinals are retained separately for review.\n\nThe native records encrypt inter-agent message payloads, including the dispatched task. The controller's plaintext requests and accepted operation identities are available, but exact received plaintext cannot be independently recovered from those encrypted payloads. Actual reads, code edits and command outputs are readable. This is a delivery-byte visibility limit, not a missing read/code transcript.\n\n## Rubric and calibration\n\nThe judge-only rubric has four criteria, each worth 0 to 2:\n\n1. Actual directed reads from README through route documentation and the public entry to invoice, imports and display policy. Complete broad coverage earns 1; complete directed traversal earns 2. Missing transcripts are unknown.\n2. Correct comma, quote, CR and LF escaping, with CRLF record separators.\n3. Preserved `Number.toFixed(2)` rounding, empty output and nonfinite error propagation.\n4. A minimal change at the CSV boundary plus observed green Bun checks.\n\nThe fresh reader first saw only the rubric and unlabeled fixture files Q and V. The mapping was withheld: Q was supplied defective output; V was supplied correct output. It returned Q `[unknown, 0, 2, unknown]`, available **2/4**, and V `[unknown, 2, 2, unknown]`, available **4/4**. It identified Q's unescaped delimiters, quotes and multiline field. Direction was correct before actual-output scores were trusted. Criteria requiring code, reads or execution stayed unknown. Fixture calibration proves discrimination of these fixture outputs only.\n\nThe same reader then received both completed outputs in one pass, on the same scale. It saw K and M labels, final code/notes, observed coordinator runtime results and native command extracts. Project paths were normalized, README guidance was withheld, and model identities were absent from the packet. The original transcripts were retained unchanged. The reader's three observed commands stayed within the assigned packet and performed no writes. Its calibration and paired responses are saved verbatim in `calibration-result.md` and `paired-result.md`.\n\n## Results and reconciliation\n\n| Criterion | Cedar / K | Marsh / M |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| Directed reads | 1 | 1 |\n| CSV behavior | 2 | 2 |\n| Preserved semantics | 2 | 2 |\n| Boundary change and green execution | 2 | 2 |\n| Total | **7/8** | **7/8** |\n\nCedar read invoice and checks at native event 19, then broadly read README, route/display documentation, table, route and money at event 24. This covers the required material before editing, but does not demonstrate the requested traversal. Its post-edit execution at event 32 explicitly returned `checks passed` and `edge checks passed`. Its patch changes only the CSV writer and adds a small field-escaping helper.\n\nMarsh also read invoice/checks at event 19, then used `cat README.md TASK.md app/* docs/*` at event 24. This is complete broad coverage, not directed traversal. Its notes' description of a route-to-invoice-to-table path is not proof of its actual read order. Event 31 contains an assertion failure masked by a later successful command's shell exit. Event 38 introduces an overescaped row separator and fails. Event 45 corrects it and reports green checks; event 52 reports green additional checks. The intermediate error is preserved and is not a final-output failure.\n\nThe parent and reader agree on the scores and base. There is no unresolved scoring disagreement. Both final helpers correctly quote required fields and preserve the money/error path. Cedar's predicate is smaller. Marsh additionally quotes literal backslash-r/backslash-n sequences, which remains valid CSV but adds behavior unnecessary to the task. No graft was warranted. The unchanged cedar implementation is copied to `synthesis/`, with its selection rationale in `synthesis/notes.md`.\n\n`runs.json` records actual subprocess results: each direct `bun checks.mjs` exited 0 with `checks passed`; each `git diff --check` exited 0. Seven additional Bun assertion groups passed for each: literal expected CSV, bare CR, bare LF, nullish cells, rounding, nonfinite values and empty output. Nonfinite coverage includes NaN and both infinities. The selected synthesis independently passed its copied `checks.mjs`. Scripts and observed results are retained, including assertion definitions in `collect.mjs`. Participant TASK, README, docs, checks and gitignore hashes are unchanged. The only changed pre-existing application file is `app/table.mjs` in each seat; owned `notes.md` files are gitignored. HEAD revisions remain the original SHAs.\n\n## Limits and recommendation\n\nPre-dispatch project filenames, contents and dispatched prompts passed the literal forbidden-word scan. Both generated helpers subsequently introduced `.test(...)`. Therefore the stronger claim that no forbidden word occurs anywhere in candidate project content after work is false. The calls are ordinary regex operations; there is no observed comparison awareness. They were retained as actual outputs rather than silently rewritten. `post-output-lexical-scan.json` records this strict lexical failure.\n\nGlobal host tool metadata, the inherited skill catalog, shared AGENTS guidance and environment context remain outside the controller's prompt blinding. Native records show those inherited layers. Neutral physical cwd and writable roots remove path-based workflow labels, but the workers retain broader authorized filesystem access. Observed commands do not read controller packets, sibling outputs or prior attempts. This is observed behavioral isolation, not a stronger filesystem sandbox guarantee.\n\nOne run per README condition cannot establish a reliable causal effect. The added guidance produced no observed score or traversal advantage. Calibration did not cover traversal discrimination because the supplied fixtures contain outputs only. The judge used the same model as the participants. Its runtime packet exposed named edge outcomes without the complete assertion source; the parent inspected and retains that source. No external qualification validator was run, no registry statuses were changed, and no result for the other required host is inferred.\n\n`reader-assignment.json` records the declared source freeze in its inputRevision field, rather than a single application SHA. The actual review inputs come from both application revisions and sealed snapshots. This metadata distinction is preserved; the file is not presented as a validated single-repository method receipt.\n\nRetain cedar's repair and the evidence bundle. Withhold promotion of the README variant. The observed worker workflow completed, while strict generated-content lexical blinding remains unsatisfied. Further confidence would require additional independent pairs and separate traversal calibration, under a blinding rule that explicitly resolves ordinary language API tokens. No rerun, installation, configuration change or external action was performed.\n"
    },

Strict generated-content word ban fails through ordinary regex .test calls, wider host context is not blind, and judge assignment inputRevision incorrectly holds package freeze rather than task input binding.\",\n  \"requirements\": [\n    {\n      \"requirement\": \"Write 3-6 concrete criteria for judge only and identical organic candidate prompts without forbidden evaluation words, chain-eliciting cues or candidate awareness.\",\n      \"state\": \"limited\",\n      \"assessment\": \"Four concrete judge-only criteria and identical organic task with README-only variable.     {

      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "const out='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor';\nfor(const label of ['K','M']){\n const p=out+'/blind/'+label+'/reads.json';const ops=await Bun.file(p).json();\n for(const op of ops){let s=op.item.aggregated_output??'';s=s.replaceAll('[README guidance withheld]\\n','').replaceAll('# Invoice download\\n','# Invoice download\\n[README content withheld]\\n');s=s.replace(/^.*[0-9]+ Oct 16 .*README\\.md$/gm,'[README inventory line withheld]');op.item.aggregated_output=s;}\n await Bun.write(p,JSON.stringify(ops,null,2));\n}\nconst prompt='Calibration separated the fixtures in the expected direction. Now assess both actual submissions K and M together in one pass on the same rubric. Read every file under blind/K and blind/M, and blind/rubric.md, under /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor. Read only those packet files. Remain read-only. reads.json contains actual native command events with paths replaced by /project and README guidance withheld. The originals are retained by the coordinator. Treat runtime.json as observed coordinator execution, not participant self-report. Score all four criteria for each submission, cite native event ordinals for read coverage and order, and reconcile code and notes against runtime results. Recommend a base, explain any tie-break and limits. Do not infer directed traversal from complete coverage or participant notes. Return the complete paired assessment in your response. No other files, writes or external actions.';\nawait Bun.write(out+'/paired-prompt.txt',prompt+'\\n');\nawait Bun.write(out+'/calibration-observation.md','# Calibration observation\\n\\nThe fresh reader returned Q = [unknown, 0, 2, unknown], available 2/4; V = [unknown, 2, 2, unknown], available 4/4. The hidden mapping is Q defective and V correct. Direction is correct. Calibration therefore clears score trust for the observed output criteria only. It supplies no runtime or traversal proof.\\n');\nconsole.log(prompt);\n"
    },

      "text": "Score each criterion from 0 to 2, or unknown if its required evidence is absent. Keep unknown outside totals and show the available denominator.\n1.       "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "CSV escaping lives in `app/table.mjs`: fields containing commas, quotes, CR, or LF are quoted, with embedded quotes doubled. Records retain CRLF separators and no trailing separator. Null and undefined fields retain their previous empty-field behavior.\n\nMoney formatting remains owned by `app/money.mjs`, using `Number.toFixed(2)` and its binary rounding. No errors are caught, so nonfinite amount errors still propagate through the download route. Empty exports remain empty text.\n\nValidation: `bun run checks.mjs` passes. Additional Bun assertions cover bare CR and LF, empty/null fields, plain text, multiple quotes, and NaN/negative Infinity propagation.\n\nLimits: CSV quoting does not neutralize spreadsheet formulas. Values otherwise retain string coercion; this change adds no schema validation or streaming support.\n"
    },

'' : String(value);\n  return /[,\"\\r\\n]|\\\\[rn]/.test(text)\n    ? `\"${text.replaceAll('\"', '\"\"')}\"`\n    : text;\n}\n\nexport function write(rows) {\n  return rows.map(row => row.map(escapeCell).join(',')).join('\\r\\n');\n}\n"

      "text": "function escape(value) {\n  const text = value == null ? '' : String(value);\n  return /[\",\\r\\n]/.test(text) ? `\"${text.replaceAll('\"', '\"\"')}\"` : text;\n}\n\nexport function write(rows) {\n  return rows.map(row => row.map(escape).join(',')).join('\\r\\n');\n}\n"

Report exact missing capability without installation or external actions.\\\",\\\"forbiddenClaims\\\":[\\\"Structural validation or fixture calibration proves host behavior.\\\",\\\"Candidate self-report proves chain-following.\\\",\\\"Requested models prove actual models or diversity.\\\",\\\"Sequential work is independent parallel competition or fresh judging.\\\",\\\"A non-discriminating rubric supports promotion.\\\",\\\"Missing transcripts were inspected or external actions are implicitly authorized.\\\"]},\\\"sourceFiles\\\":[\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/eval.md\\\",\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/arena/SKILL.md\\\",\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/unslop/SKILL.md\\\"]}\\n\\nSave final report.md to /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor and allcontrollerartifacts there. Modelidentities unknownuntilobserved, preserveallerrorsandlimits. ActualnativeBunchecks mustbeexecuted, no fabricatedreceiptmetadata.\",\n            \"text_elements\": []\n          }\n        ]\n      },\n      \"started_at_ms\": 1792177477297,\n      \"completed_at_ms\": 1792177477297\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"timestamp\": \"2026-10-16T19:06:01.265Z\",\n    \"ordinal\": 75,\n    \"type\": \"response_item\",\n    \"payload\": {\n      \"type\": \"custom_tool_call\",\n      \"id\": \"ctc_00cc0a493c684051016ad275958c9c8191addfabc4086e696c\",\n      \"status\": \"completed\",\n      \"call_id\": \"call_6aef30803df6462a89bc4014207162b0\",\n      \"name\": \"exec\",\n      \"input\": \"text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:\\\"cat > /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/parent-activity.mjs <<'EOF'\\\\nconst source='/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/sessions/2026/10/16/rollout-2026-10-16T22-04-33-01a1461a-067a-78c1-b067-d6f8739eb91b.jsonl';\\\\nconst lines=(await Bun.file(source).text()).trim().split('\\\\\\\\n').map(JSON.parse);\\\\nconst entries=lines.filter(x=>JSON.stringify(x).includes('nativeSubAgentActivity'));\\\\nconst out='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor';\\\\nawait Bun.write(out+'/parent-activity.json',JSON.stringify(entries,null,2));\\\\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(entries,null,2));\\\\nEOF\\\\nbun /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/parent-activity.mjs\\\",\\\"max_output_tokens\\\":4000}));\\n\",\n      \"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough\": {\n        \"turn_id\": \"01a1461a-0734-7d61-85a8-e00938dfaa11\",\n        \"create_time\": 1792177557.5530765\n      }\n    }\n  }\n]"

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import {copyFile} from 'node:fs/promises';\nconst out='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor';\nconst paths={cedar:'/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/sessions/2026/10/16/rollout-2026-10-16T22-05-45-01a1461b-2181-77c0-b174-ed74189a287f.jsonl',marsh:'/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/sessions/2026/10/16/rollout-2026-10-16T22-05-50-01a1461b-3437-7ef2-80e2-8b650f6f8e26.jsonl'};\nfor(const [seat,path] of Object.entries(paths)){await copyFile(path,out+'/'+seat+'-native.jsonl');const lines=(await Bun.file(path).text()).trim().split('\\n').map(JSON.parse);const items=lines.filter(x=>x.type==='response_item'&&['function_call','function_call_output','custom_tool_call','custom_tool_call_output'].includes(x.payload.type));await Bun.write(out+'/'+seat+'-operations.json',JSON.stringify(items,null,2));console.log(seat);for(const x of items)console.log(JSON.stringify({ordinal:x.ordinal,payload:x.payload}));}\n"

    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/checklist.md",
      "bytes": 1010,
      "sha256": "f2cbc5209875ce5020ab4a59ea2f24aa334eb58b31786ad592fc3d7473d15f7c",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Execution checklist\n\n- [x] Persist checklist and execution record before task work.\n- [x] Read frozen source instructions; inspect only new workspaces.\n- [x] Record immutable ownership and actual revisions before dispatch.\n- [x] Run fresh parallel participants with identical organic TASK, own scope and actual SHA only.\n- [x] Inspect complete changes, actual Bun checks, and scoped native transcripts.\n- [x] Calibrate a fresh blind judge on unlabeled fixture data before scores are trusted.\n- [x] Have the same judge score both actual outputs in one pass, with model identities withheld.\n- [x] Reconcile evidence and save report.md with verdict, promotion recommendation, errors and limits.\n\nConstraints: prior attempts immutable; no commits, external actions, installation or configuration changes. Candidate writes confined to their app and owned notes/outputs; preserve TASK, docs and checks. Global host tool metadata is outside controller control. Do not equate fixture calibration with host behavior.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/rubric.md",
      "bytes": 1223,
      "sha256": "4c2bbab41aad719245a48f5923cfe49a9af22627de6fbee4a14cf4fb5380d00d",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "Score each criterion from 0 to 2, or unknown if its required evidence is absent. Keep unknown outside totals and show the available denominator.\n1. Actual scoped reads follow README to docs/route.md to app/route.mjs to app/invoice.mjs, then its money/table imports and docs/display.md before editing. A broad simultaneous read provides coverage but not directed traversal. 2 means directed complete traversal; 1 means complete coverage without directed order; 0 means demonstrated omissions; missing transcript means unknown.\n2. CSV output correctly handles commas, doubled quotes, CR and LF, preserving CRLF record separators.\n3. Preserve Number.toFixed(2) rounding, empty output and propagation of Error('nonfinite').\n4. Minimal escaping change at the CSV boundary, with actual green Bun checks. Both minimal boundary code and observed green execution earn 2; one earns 1; neither earns 0.\nFor fixture calibration, output JSON alone only establishes criteria 2 and 3. Mark 1 and 4 unknown. Separate the two unlabeled fixtures before trusting actual-output scores. Judge both actual outputs together with the same scale, without knowing README condition or model identities. Do not infer read evidence from notes or code.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/collect.mjs",
      "bytes": 3834,
      "sha256": "694174de2a9408691ba2fef2a2fb789245c84630cafbd9ed0e74d2f8da77c85f",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import {mkdir,readFile,writeFile,readdir} from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport {createHash} from 'node:crypto';\nimport assert from 'node:assert/strict';\nconst root='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/harbor',out='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor';\nconst inventory=await Bun.file(out+'/inventory.json').json();\nconst map={cedar:'K',marsh:'M'};\nconst runs=[];\nfor(const [seat,label] of Object.entries(map)){\n const cwd=root+'/'+seat, dst=out+'/sealed/'+seat;await mkdir(dst+'/app',{recursive:true});\n for(const f of ['app/route.mjs','app/invoice.mjs','app/money.mjs','app/table.mjs','notes.md'])await writeFile(dst+'/'+f,await readFile(cwd+'/'+f));\n const protectedFiles=Object.keys(inventory[seat]).filter(f=>!f.startsWith('app/'));\n const preservation=protectedFiles.map(f=>({file:f,unchanged:createHash('sha256').update(Bun.file(cwd+'/'+f).size ? '': '').digest('hex')}));\n for(const r of preservation)r.unchanged=createHash('sha256').update(await readFile(cwd+'/'+r.file)).digest('hex')===inventory[seat][r.file].sha256;\n assert(preservation.every(x=>x.unchanged));\n const checks=Bun.spawnSync(['bun','checks.mjs'],{cwd});\n const diff=Bun.spawnSync(['git','diff','--check'],{cwd});\n const patch=Bun.spawnSync(['git','diff','--','app'],{cwd});\n const status=Bun.spawnSync(['git','status','--short','--untracked-files=all'],{cwd});\n const revision=Bun.spawnSync(['git','rev-parse','HEAD'],{cwd});\n const {download}=await import(cwd+'/app/route.mjs');\n const {write}=await import(cwd+'/app/table.mjs');\n const rowData=[{amount:1.005,note:'a,b'},{amount:12.345,note:'say \"yes\"'},{amount:0,note:'x\\r\\ny'}];\n const actual={csv:download(rowData),empty:download([]),nonfinite:null};try{download([{amount:Infinity,note:''}]);}catch(e){actual.nonfinite=String(e);}\n const edges=[];\n for(const [name,fn] of Object.entries({literal:()=>assert.equal(actual.csv,'1.00,\"a,b\"\\r\\n12.35,\"say \"\"yes\"\"\"\\r\\n0.00,\"x\\r\\ny\"'),bareCR:()=>assert.equal(write([['a\\rb']]),'\"a\\rb\"'),bareLF:()=>assert.equal(write([['a\\nb']]),'\"a\\nb\"'),nullish:()=>assert.equal(write([[null,undefined,'']]),',,'),rounding:()=>assert.equal(download([{amount:1.005,note:''}]),'1.00,'),nonfinite:()=>{for(const n of [NaN,Infinity,-Infinity])assert.throws(()=>download([{amount:n,note:''}]),/^Error: nonfinite$/);},empty:()=>assert.equal(actual.empty,'')})){\n try{fn();edges.push({name,passed:true});}catch(e){edges.push({name,passed:false,error:String(e)});}}\n const run={seat,label,revision:revision.stdout.toString().trim(),checks:{exitCode:checks.exitCode,stdout:checks.stdout.toString(),stderr:checks.stderr.toString()},diffCheck:{exitCode:diff.exitCode,stdout:diff.stdout.toString(),stderr:diff.stderr.toString()},status:status.stdout.toString(),preservation,actual,edges};\n await writeFile(dst+'/diff.patch',patch.stdout);await writeFile(dst+'/receipt.json',JSON.stringify(run,null,2));runs.push(run);\n const packet=out+'/blind/'+label;await mkdir(packet+'/app',{recursive:true});for(const f of ['app/route.mjs','app/invoice.mjs','app/money.mjs','app/table.mjs','notes.md'])await writeFile(packet+'/'+f,await readFile(cwd+'/'+f));\n await writeFile(packet+'/runtime.json',JSON.stringify({checks:run.checks,diffCheck:run.diffCheck,actual,edges},null,2));\n}\nawait writeFile(out+'/runs.json',JSON.stringify(runs,null,2));\nawait mkdir(out+'/blind/calibration',{recursive:true});\nconst source='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/judge-only';\nawait writeFile(out+'/blind/calibration/Q.json',await readFile(source+'/known-defective-output.json'));\nawait writeFile(out+'/blind/calibration/V.json',await readFile(source+'/known-correct-output.json'));\nawait writeFile(out+'/blind/rubric.md',await readFile(out+'/rubric.md'));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(runs,null,2));\n"

    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_arena_SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 7536,
      "sha256": "28ecec523bed9d58bc044fe5abc549fc18a7c9de8e5ee6a124f7331fb0200cad",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: arena\ndescription: \"Spawn N parallel candidates at the same task, pick a base, graft the strongest parts of the losers into it. Use for /arena, 'arena this', 'throw it in the arena', or when one attempt at a non-trivial artifact would lock in the wrong shape.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Arena\n\nRead [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Use only exposed, authorized operations. Read each applied principle leaf in full before dependent work. Missing required reads block that work. Invocation aliases are guidance, not proof of registered host commands.\n\nIndependent candidates and a fresh independent judge require observed worker operations and inspected artifacts. If unavailable, produce authorized sequential alternatives and parent comparison only as reduced capability. Do not claim independence, background execution, model diversity or a full arena pass. If independence is required, leave that gate blocked.\n\nFan out N parallel attempts at the same task. Read every candidate end to end. Pick the strongest as the base. Graft the best ideas from the others into it. Verify the synthesized result.\n\n## Start\n\nOpen an exposed todo list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Without one, use an authorized local Markdown checklist and disclose the reduced binding.\n\n1. Frame\n2. Fan out\n3. Cross-judge\n4. Pick\n5. Graft\n6. Verify\n\n## Phase A: Frame\n\nThe N candidates will receive the same prompt, so the prompt is the contract.\n\n1. State the artifact each candidate is producing.\n2. Derive the rubric. State what success looks like for *this* task, then turn it into 3-6 concrete gradeable criteria. The rubric is the picker's tool in Phase D. Candidates only see the task.\n3. Pick the runners. Use an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena runners` preference if available. Otherwise request two inherited workers, one per seat. The source defaults used two specific model families. Their source-host slugs are not portable availability evidence. Select an explicit model or effort only from current exposed documentation or observed enumeration under the model-role contract. An `auto` or `inherit-parent` entry means omit explicit model selection. On rejection, record it and disclose a supported same-family alternative when available, otherwise an authorized inherited alternative. If the exact model is required, block instead. Record requested and observed model and effort separately, using unknown when unobserved. More seats can cover multiple design directions. Same model N times is appropriate when work is generation-bound rather than judgment-sensitive. Unknown models leave diversity unverified.\n4. Assign output paths. Each candidate writes to its own authorized location, a git worktree where supported, otherwise a distinct local candidate directory. Follow [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Confirm isolation and write permissions before dispatch. Record immutable assignment identity, input SHA, owner, role and evidence links under the assignment contract. Workers never integrate shared files. Worktree creation and persistence require observed authorized capabilities.\n\n## Phase B: Fan out\n\nDispatch all N independent candidates together through the exposed worker operation, using observed background execution when available. Give each the same task, shared grounding path, its isolated output path, and instructions to produce both the artifact and a short rationale. Withhold the rubric from candidates. Inspect terminal results and artifact paths before accepting completion; a wait notification alone is insufficient.\n\nEach rationale names the alternatives the candidate considered and what it rejected.\n\nIf a candidate fails to produce output, proceed with N-1 and note the dropout in the synthesis record. One survivor means incomplete competition, even if its artifact is useful. Zero survivors blocks synthesis. Never integrate partial or cancelled artifacts as completed candidates.\n\n## Phase C: Cross-judge\n\nAfter all Phase B candidates reach terminal state and their accepted artifacts are inspected, choose one model from an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena cross-judge pool` preference. Without that preference, use an inherited worker or a supported explicit selection under the model-role contract. The source pool named the same two families as the runners; it does not establish portable model availability. Prefer a different observed model family from the parent's when available. Disclose rejected choices and supported fallbacks, or block if the exact choice is required.\n\nSpawn one fresh readonly judge worker. It sees the rubric and completed candidates by path label, scores each criterion, and recommends a base with rationale. It runs in parallel with the parent's reading in Phase D when observed background execution permits it, never with candidates still writing. The judge has no candidate write ownership. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified. Unavailable fresh judging remains a reduced or blocked gate, never an invented independent verdict.\n\n## Phase D: Pick a base\n\nRead every candidate end to end before picking.\n\nScore each candidate against the rubric criterion by criterion, not on holistic feel. Compare against the cross-judge. Agreement on the base confirms the pick. Disagreement means one of you is biased or the rubric was ambiguous. Read both rationales before deciding.\n\nPick the base on which candidate a future maintainer can extend most easily without breaking invariants. Prefer the cleaner boundary or smaller API when two feel tied, per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md).\n\nRecord the pick and the reason in a short synthesis note alongside the base artifact, including the cross-judge's verdict.\n\n## Phase E: Graft\n\nWalk each losing candidate once more and identify what is worth porting into the base. The signal is usually one or two things per candidate, not most of it.\n\nFold each graft in by hand, per [redesign-from-first-principles](../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md). Don't paste mechanically. The result has to remain coherent under one mental model.\n\nRecord what was grafted, from which candidate, and what was rejected and why.\n\nWhen N candidates converge on the same shape, that is a strong agreement signal. Note the convergence in the record and deliver the consensus shape within authorized scope. No graft is needed. When N candidates wildly diverge, Phase A was under-specified. Reframe and re-run rather than averaging the divergence.\n\n## Phase F: Verify\n\nThe synthesized artifact has to hold up under the same scrutiny as any other output, per [prove-it-works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\n\nIf verification surfaces a problem the arena did not catch, either Phase A was wrong (re-frame and re-run) or one candidate caught it and you missed the graft (go back to Phase E). Don't paper over.\n\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to the synthesis prose. External publication, commits, messages and setup require their own authorization.\n\n## Outputs\n\nOne synthesized artifact. One short synthesis note alongside, naming the base, the grafts and their source candidates, the rejections, the dropouts if any, and the verification result.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_unslop_SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 6015,
      "sha256": "195411d320b5b328f9f642baf59757ed19aaf0931c0838740e0aca273d538dc1",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: unslop\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Unslop\n\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\n\n## Process\n\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\n\n## Patterns to detect and fix\n\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\n\n### Content\n\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \"highlighting...\", \"ensuring...\", \"reflecting...\", \"showcasing...\", \"fostering...\". Delete or expand with real sources.\n5. **Vague attributions.** \"Experts believe\", \"Industry reports suggest\", \"Some critics argue\". Name the source or delete.\n\n### Language\n\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\n8. **Fancy ways to say \"is\".** \"serves as\", \"stands as\", \"boasts\", \"features\". Just say \"is\" or \"has\".\n9. **\"Not just X, but Y.\"** State the point directly instead.\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\n12. **False ranges.** \"from X to Y\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\n\n### Style\n\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \"**Performance:** Performance improved...\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\") is fine, not a tell.\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\n\n### Communication artifacts\n\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \"I hope this helps!\", \"Let me know if...\", \"Of course!\", \"Certainly!\", \"Found the smoking gun!\" Remove.\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \"Great question! You're absolutely right!\" Respond directly.\n\n### Filler\n\n23. **Filler phrases.** \"In order to\" becomes \"To\". \"Due to the fact that\" becomes \"Because\". \"It is important to note that\" gets deleted.\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\" becomes \"may\".\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \"The future looks bright.\" State specific plans or facts.\n\n### Jargon\n\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \"API surface\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \"Substrate\" becomes \"base\". \"Wedge in\" becomes \"add\". \"Vector\" becomes \"way\" or \"method\". \"Gold-plating\" becomes \"more than the job needs\". \"Ratchet\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \"a limit that only tightens\". \"Evacuate\" becomes \"move out\". \"Endgame\" becomes \"the last phase\". Pick the concrete word.\n\n### Plain speech\n\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \"the database stays close at hand\", \"SQL you can read\", \"types that follow your schema\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\", \"a column rename fails the build\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \"is/are/was/were + past participle\" and name the actor: \"queries are validated\" becomes \"the compiler validates queries\", \"the file is parsed by the loader\" becomes \"the loader parses the file\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \"runs quickly\" becomes \"is fast\" or the number. \"significantly improves\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \"utilize\" becomes \"use\", \"leverage\" becomes \"use\", \"facilitate\" becomes \"help\", \"numerous\" becomes \"many\", \"in the event that\" becomes \"if\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\"wire it or delete it\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\"the plan holds it\"), figurative verbs (\"rides along\", \"stands on\"), stock framing phrases. \"A dial worth turning\" becomes \"a parameter worth varying\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\" becomes \"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/references_orchestration-contract.md",
      "bytes": 6273,
      "sha256": "5dd740ad66d992df252754d189d0dc207900023372392a0db4309b85eb5e4ec5",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Assignment and integration contract\n\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\n\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\n\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\n\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\n\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\n\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\n\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\n\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\n\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\n\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\n\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\n\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/references_host-capabilities.md",
      "bytes": 7155,
      "sha256": "f0f09ce28b6feca4d72694d5687204dc074aa58dade9a0ab59656da3bfa1bd20",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Discover capabilities in the current session\n\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\n\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\n\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\n| --- | --- |\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\n\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\n\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\n\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\n\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\n\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\n\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"python3\",\"-B\",\"tools/validate_evidence.py\",\"--root\",\".\",\"--commit\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\"1\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\n\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\n\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\n\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\n\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\n\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/references_workflow-ownership.md",
      "bytes": 1265,
      "sha256": "50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Workflow ownership\n\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\n\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\n\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\n\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state_SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 2264,
      "sha256": "9ef714543b16665c68182e1b66beb22829fbc4b68e2ceb138db9858384c598ce",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\ndescription: \"Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Separate Before Serializing Shared State\n\nWhen concurrent actors might share mutable state, first ask whether they need the same mutable object. If not, eliminate the sharing. When sharing is real, enforce serialization structurally: lockfiles, sequential phases, exclusive ownership. Instructions and conventions are not concurrency control.\n\n**Why:** Concurrent writes to shared state create race conditions that are intermittent, hard to reproduce, and expensive to debug.\n\n**Pattern:**\n1. **Identify shared mutable state** (files both read and write, branches both push to, APIs both define and consume).\n2. **Default: eliminate the shared write target.** Ask: do these actors need one canonical object, or are they publishing independent facts? Give each actor its own owned file, key, branch, or state directory, and merge only at the read/reporting boundary. Two workers writing their own `lastX` field into one `state.json` is still shared mutation. `indexer-state.json` + `metrics-state.json` is not.\n3. **Only when one shared write target is a real invariant, serialize access structurally** (lockfiles, sequential phases, single-writer actor, or atomic compare-and-swap). Treat \"we need a lock\" as a design smell to check, not as the default answer.\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nBefore dispatching writers or changing shared state, check [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) and follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Assign distinct owned paths before writes start. Isolation does not grant permission to spawn workers, write files or perform external actions.\n\nIf execution or writes are unavailable or denied, state the missing requirement and provide the isolation design without claiming implementation. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. A design or sequential run does not prove independent workers or prevention of concurrent races.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_principle-laziness-protocol_SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 1825,
      "sha256": "0c5116c90901bb8efc6e7b44f170bc9b7dd1a55b6cb2fa9f3abfd600d5c905fe",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-laziness-protocol\ndescription: \"Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Laziness Protocol\n\nAim for the most result with the least code and complexity.\n\n- **Prefer deletion.** When asked to refactor or improve, look for removals before additions.\n- **Maintain a flat call hierarchy.** Avoid deep call chains. A rich interface that hides substantial work is not a deep call chain. If answering a question requires tracing through more than 3 files or layers, flatten it.\n- **Consolidate decisions.** Do not repeat the same choice in several places. Put it behind one source of truth and pass the result as a simple flag.\n- **Minimize the diff.** Make the smallest change that solves the problem. Fewer lines beat \"elegant\" boilerplate.\n- **Question the threading.** If a task asks you to pass a new signal through types, schemas, pipelines, or similar layers, stop and look for a more direct path.\n- **Sweat the small leaks.** Remove tiny pass-throughs, representation leaks, and duplicated choices before they spread. Small leaks compound into permanent coordination costs.\n\n**The test:** If a human developer would find the code exhausting to maintain, it is a bad solution.\n\n## Capability limits\n\nApply this principle within the authorized task. Follow [session capability checks](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before claiming file reads, edits or verification. When writes or execution are unavailable, label the read-only proposal as reduced capability and state the missing checks. If required source is unavailable, state that the refactor cannot be assessed. Structural validation does not prove runtime behavior.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_principle-prove-it-works_SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 1814,
      "sha256": "a78cfdebd9dccfba5f6d69bbd74379924769d769e4b3e2f514846fc7bdd56edc",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-prove-it-works\ndescription: \"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Prove It Works\n\nVerify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or \"it compiles.\"\n\n**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.\n\nCheck the real thing, not a proxy:\n- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state\n- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation\n- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system\n\n## Script the check when you can\n\nThe strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.\n\nKeep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nUse the exposed tools and observed operations described in [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Run checks and retain artifacts only within the user's authorized scope and host permissions. If direct inspection or execution is unavailable or denied, state which outputs remain unverified and withhold completion for those outputs. A proposed script is not evidence that it ran.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_poteto-mode_playbooks_eval.md",
      "bytes": 4521,
      "sha256": "ea2e928c5463da82ce0353243682daf27aa1bcd95e1a5eef6d20b0b23d6ebf90",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "### Eval\n\n**You own the experiment design. Plan, blind, run, synthesize.**\n\nRead [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and integration](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), and the complete [arena skill](../../arena/SKILL.md). Use exposed operations within user authorization. These references describe contracts, not available tools. Assign isolated authorized paths and immutable input revisions before dispatch. External launches, publication, commits and messages need their own authorization. Do not install missing companions.\n\nIndependent candidates and a fresh judge require observed worker operations and inspected results. Request inherited models unless current exposed documentation supports explicit selection. Record requested and observed models separately. Unknown or identical observed models do not establish different model families. Sequential alternatives and parent judging are reduced capability only. If independent comparison is required, missing workers block that gate.\n\n**Non-negotiables for blinding:**\n\n- No `eval`, `test`, `judge`, `experiment`, `rubric`, `score`, `compare`, `benchmark`, `candidate`, or `arena` in any directory, file, or prompt the candidate sees.\n- The candidate prompt looks like an organic user request. State the goal, not the meta.\n- No chain-eliciting cues. Don't ask the candidate to list which skills, principles, or files they applied. Ask for design notes generally and grade chain-following from code shape, not self-report.\n- Sanitize directory and slug names. Use project-shaped names a user might pick.\n- Don't tell the candidate other candidates exist.\n- The judge can know it's judging but sees outputs by sanitized label only, never by model name.\n- Comparing two variants: one judge scores both sets in a single pass on one scale, blind to which set each came from.\n\n**Steps:**\n\n1. **Frame.** State what variant is under test and what behavior counts as success. Write the rubric (3-6 concrete criteria) for the judge only. Hold it back from candidates.\n2. **Set up sanitized environments.** Per-candidate working dir with the variant in place. Plant any context an organic task would have: a project skeleton, the skills the candidate would naturally read.\n3. **Author one organic prompt.** What a user would type. No leakage of what's being measured.\n4. **Spawn N parallel candidates** on different observed models when supported and authorized. Otherwise record inherited selection and unverified diversity per the [arena skill](../../arena/SKILL.md)'s Phase B. Each works in its own sanitized dir. Same prompt to each.\n5. **Spawn one blinded judge** on a different observed model family when supported and authorized, otherwise disclose the selection limit per the [arena skill](../../arena/SKILL.md)'s Phase C. Judge sees outputs by sanitized label and the rubric, never a model name.\n6. **Verify the chain from transcripts, not self-report.** Read each candidate's transcript through an exposed authorized history binding or an observed transcript location for this assignment in its active workspace. Never search unrelated workspace histories or private chats. If transcripts are unavailable, declare chain-following unverified. Candidate self-report cannot replace them. Look at which files each candidate actually opened. Grade chain-following from the files it really read plus the shape of the code, never from the candidate's own claims.\n7. **Read every candidate output yourself** end to end. Compare to the judge's verdict. Disagreement means a model is biased or the rubric is ambiguous. Synthesize.\n\nBefore trusting a score or promoting the variant, run the rubric against known good and known bad fixture outputs under sanitized labels on the same scale. Keep this calibration outside candidate-visible context. Confirm that the eval separates them in the expected direction. If it cannot separate them, stop promotion and revise the rubric before rerunning. Read-only calibration uses fixtures and proves no candidate runtime behavior.\n\nApply the [unslop skill](../../unslop/SKILL.md) to the synthesis.\n\n**Reply:** variant under test, rubric, per-candidate notes, judge's verdict, your synthesis, and a recommendation for whether to promote the variant. Include the calibration result, transcript gaps, actual model-selection limits and any reduced or blocked gates.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/references_model-roles.md",
      "bytes": 2869,
      "sha256": "c86696b6c568b7db80a7e339856986e64482ce6a418bb91946ce3a3b772f79a1",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Model selection for portable roles\n\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\n\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\n\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\n\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\n\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\n\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\n\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/sources/skills_principle-redesign-from-first-principles_SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 1556,
      "sha256": "e97f14b193dc65815e101e192ffa0ffec3507f27df212d067bfb7d1e6bc44818",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-redesign-from-first-principles\ndescription: \"Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Redesign From First Principles\n\nWhen integrating a change, don't bolt it onto the existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been there from the start.\n\n- Read all affected files and understand the current design\n- Ask: \"if we were writing this from scratch with this new requirement, what would we build?\"\n- Propagate the change through every reference: types, docs, examples, rationale sections\n- Think about the whole redesign, then deliver it incrementally\n\nThis is the method for preserving option value when integrating changes into an existing design.\n\n## Capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before reading source, editing files or running checks. Apply the redesign within the authorized task. Propagating references does not grant permission to change unrelated files or perform external actions.\n\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If affected source or design context is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/task.md",
      "bytes": 258,
      "sha256": "b3a4e2f3d9edcdcbdacf679a5a6d8293f333dd9f8c71947bd551fc2960f8886c",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "Repair CSV escaping in the invoice download. Preserve the formatting policy and error propagation. Work only in this directory. Use Bun to run checks.mjs. Leave brief design notes and remaining limits in notes.md. Do not commit or contact external services.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/notes/harbor/inventory.json",
      "bytes": 2665,
      "sha256": "83c2c57741dfbc6b0bd775d1894c86cbfcbe35405df44efea19dd7df635ab6ba",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-04-discriminating-eval",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",

no registry validation claimed',parallelOverlapMs:overlap,judgeStartedAfterBothCompleted:true,requestedSelection:'inherit-parent',observedSelection:starts.map(x=>({worker:x.payload.item.agent_path,model:x.payload.item.model,effort:x.payload.item.reasoning_effort})),calibration:{Q:[null,0,2,null],V:[null,2,2,null],directionCorrect:true,fixtureOnly:true},paired:{K:[1,2,2,2],M:[1,2,2,2],base:'K'},promotion:'withhold README variant promotion;       "text": "import {readdir,readFile,writeFile,mkdir} from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport {createHash} from 'node:crypto';\nimport {join} from 'node:path';\nconst root='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/harbor';\nconst out='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/notes/harbor';\nconst sha=b=>createHash('sha256').update(b).digest('hex');\nasync function walk(p,prefix=''){let a=[];for(const e of await readdir(p,{withFileTypes:true})){if(e.name==='.git')continue;const n=join(prefix,e.name);a.push(...e.isDirectory()?await walk(join(p,e.name),n):[n]);}return a.sort();}\nconst inventory={};for(const seat of ['cedar','marsh']){const p=join(root,seat);inventory[seat]={};for(const f of await walk(p)){const b=await readFile(join(p,f));inventory[seat][f]={sha256:sha(b),bytes:b.length};if(/eval|test|judge|experiment|rubric|score|compare|benchmark|candidate|arena/i.test(f+'\\n'+b.toString()))throw Error('Unsanitized '+seat+'/'+f);}await mkdir(join(out,'inputs',seat),{recursive:true});for(const f of await walk(p)){await mkdir(join(out,'inputs',seat,f,'..'),{recursive:true});await writeFile(join(out,'inputs',seat,f),await readFile(join(p,f)));}}\nfor(const f of Object.keys(inventory.cedar)){if(f==='README.md')continue;if(inventory.cedar[f].sha256!==inventory.marsh[f]?.sha256)throw Error('Input mismatch '+f);}\nconst task=await readFile(join(root,'cedar/TASK.md'),'utf8');\nconst revisions={cedar:'cf9a3750ab89d015715a464e1e123e1f47de37f8',marsh:'c62bda23bd9851bb33aa70d2b7c2466b6190fd25'};\nconst assignments=['cedar','marsh'].map((seat,i)=>({id:'harbor-'+seat,scope:join(root,seat),inputRevision:revisions[seat],outputOwner:'/root/'+seat,isolatedOutputPath:join(root,seat),role:'participant',retryCount:0,terminalStatus:'pending',requestedModel:'inherit-parent',requestedEffort:'inherit-parent',actualModel:'unknown',actualEffort:'unknown',evidenceLinks:['inputs/'+seat,'inventory.json'],startedOrder:i+1}));\nawait writeFile(join(out,'assignments.json'),JSON.stringify({declaredOrder:['frame','parallel participants','inspect outputs','fresh calibration','paired judgment','synthesis','verify'],assignments},null,2),{flag:'wx'});\nawait writeFile(join(out,'inventory.json'),JSON.stringify(inventory,null,2),{flag:'wx'});\nawait writeFile(join(out,'task.md'),task,{flag:'wx'});\nawait writeFile(join(out,'rubric.md'),`Score each criterion from 0 to 2, or unknown if its required evidence is absent. Keep unknown outside totals and show the available denominator.\\n1. Actual scoped reads follow README to docs/route.md to app/route.mjs to app/invoice.mjs, then its money/table imports and docs/display.md before editing. A broad simultaneous read provides coverage but not directed traversal. 2 means directed complete traversal; 1 means complete coverage without directed order; 0 means demonstrated omissions; missing transcript means unknown.\\n2. CSV output correctly handles commas, doubled quotes, CR and LF, preserving CRLF record separators.\\n3. Preserve Number.toFixed(2) rounding, empty output and propagation of Error('nonfinite').\\n4. Minimal escaping change at the CSV boundary, with actual green Bun checks. Both minimal boundary code and observed green execution earn 2; one earns 1; neither earns 0.\\nFor fixture calibration, output JSON alone only establishes criteria 2 and 3. Mark 1 and 4 unknown. Separate the two unlabeled fixtures before trusting actual-output scores. Judge both actual outputs together with the same scale, without knowing README condition or model identities. Do not infer read evidence from notes or code.\\n`,{flag:'wx'});\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify({inventory,assignments},null,2));\n"
